    1. At first glance, the Corrupted Monk seems to behave much like the one you fought in the Ashina Depths. The only noticeable difference to see here is that this isn't a phantom, and so Divine Confetti will do you no favors here - and also that the Corrupted Monk has three health bars.
    2. Much like the first fight, the Corrupted Monk uses her naginata to slam down with attacks, and can also do multiple sweeping slice combos that have a rhythm you'll need to get down to properly deflect attacks. Many times, the Corrupted Monk will end a combo string with either a perilous thrust or low sweep - so keep an eye on the movements of the blade to see when she holds it straight and close, or holds it low off to the side to prepare to spin in circles.
    3. There are also several tree branches along the bridge of this boss arena - and you can use them to get around quickly if boxed into a corner, but don't rely too much as she still has enough reach to hit you with her long naginata.
    4. On the second health bar, things get noticeably different. A black and white mist fog will envelop the arena as the Corrupted Monk disappears, only to reappear as three different phantoms that will perform one attack before disappearing.
    5. The first phantom will step forward with a sweeping slash, the second phantom will appear nearby with an overhead strike, and the third phantom will appear also nearby for a jumping slash. This will repeat at least three times, and can come from any angle. Since you'll only have a few moments to react, you have a couple of options: Try and spin the camera and deflect the
    6. three attacks - sprint up and down the length of the bridge to outrun their attacks, or grapple between branches to avoid the attacks.
    7. Each has their own strengths and flaws - and no method is truly perfect, though you can also pull out the Loaded Umbrella and turtle up and hope your posture lasts long enough to shield up three separate times. Just be aware that grappling doesn't mean you'll be immune, and sprinting can set you in the path of an appearing phantom.
    8. Other than that, the second part of this fight is still mostly about watching for the slashes and slowly building up posture whenever possible by taking every opportunity to deflect and counter. It can be pretty hard to tell when the monk is blocking blows, but know that attacks from behind cannot be blocked by her - so take that into account.
    9. Finally, when you reach the third phase, the Undying Centipede will show itself to put a new spin on the final phase. In addition to everything you've faced before, the Corrupted Monk has a new unblockable attack where she begins to shriek as the centipede spews gunk in a front- facing arc that does extreme terror damage. If you see her start to retch and scream, quickly dodge around behind and get your hits in where you can.
    10. Another move she brings in from the previous fight is the whirlwind of spinning attacks that are spaced but deal a ton of damage and can be hard to reliably deflect - but difficult to avoid as to the wide range of movement she can cover while spinning.
    11. Thankfully, the centipede can be her biggest undoing, as it allows for you to deal a lot of damage without her being able to block while spewing gunk. This can let you lower her health to the point that her posture barely recovers, and then you can work on deflecting everything you can to finally put her and the centipede down, and sever the Corrupted Monk's immortality.
    12. Defeating her for good will earn the Memory: True Monk, as well as the Dragon's Tally Board. This item will increase the wares that all basic merchants sell, adding an infinite amount of Divine Confetti, Eel Livers, Snap Seeds, Black Gunpowder, and Scrap Iron!

    Alternate Ending Questline - Return

    Video Guide: True Ending Questline

    1. At this point in the game, you can fully complete a secret side quest that has become available since meeting the Divine Child of the Rejuvenating Waters at Senpou Temple. If you are looking into the possibility of finding a different way out other than the Immortal Severance, you may want to look into this side quest.
    2. It begins after getting Rice from the Divine Child. If you visit her often after eating the Rice or giving it to the Old Crone, you can eventually return to the Inner Sanctum to find the Divine Child in pain and distress.
    3. After speaking with her, the Divine Child will request food - a Persimmon. You can find these either by visiting the Temple Memorial Mob below the Shugendo Sculptor's Idol, or killing Monkeys around the Temple Grounds.
    4. Give the Divine Child a Persimmon, and she'll thank you not only with Rice, but also give you Rice for Kuro - a Key Item gift that she wants you to give to the young Lord.
    5. Return to Lord Kuro (which must be done BEFORE you defeat the boss of the Fountainhead Palace), and give him the Rice for Kuro. Rest once and speak to him and he'll give you two helpings of Sweet Rice (you don't have to eat it in front of him if you don't want to).
    6. Head back to the Inner Sanctum and let the Divine Child know of Lord Kuro's reaction. If you rest once more, you'll find her back turned to the entrance and facing the shrine as she speaks aloud to the spirits of her friends. At this point, you need to give her a specific item - the Holy Tome: Infested. There are two ways to encounter this tome:
    7. ▪ If you had visited Senpou Temple BEFORE defeating Genichiro, you could find the Head Priest at the Main Hall of the temple, and speaking to him he would give you the tome.
    8. ▪ If you visited Senpou Temple AFTER defeating Genichiro, the only place to find it is at the bottom of the pond in the Temple Grounds, which requires the Mibu Breathing Technique to reach.
    9. Once you have given her the Holy Tome and given Kuro the rice, you can rest and find that the Divine Child has disappeared from the Inner Sanctum. In fact, she has moved to the Halls of Illusion - the place where you fought the Folding Screen Monkeys - and has moved to where the monk was standing in front of the giant tree.
    10. Speaking to her here, she thinks there may be another way to help Lord Kuro beyond the Immortal Severance - but needs a specific book from the High Priest of Senpou Temple.
    11. Since the High Priest has left the Main Hall, you can now find his body in the small cave outside the Main Hall leading to the cliffisde pagoda where the Senpou Esoteric Text was (he's the only corpse in the cave wearing purple robes). Loot his body, and you'll gain the Holy Chapter: Dragon's Return.
    12. Rest and return to the Inner Sanctum to give the Divine Child this book, and she will find a new way to help the Divine Dragon and its immortality curse to return to where it came from. This will require the Divine Child to eat the fruits of the Great Serpent.
    13. Our walkthrough has previously covered how to get each of these key items, and you can find them with our guides - How to Get the Fresh Serpent Viscera, and How to Get the Dried Serpent Viscera.
    14. Give the Divine Child both of these items, and then leave her to eat them and suffer the effects. Resting will let you eavesdrop on the closed door to the sanctum where you can listen to her writhe in pain. However, she will not open the doors until you have defeated the Corrupted Monk at the Fountainhead Palace.
    15. Now that you've done this, you can return to find her back to normal, and she will bestow upon you Frozen Tears, which can be used alongside the Divine Dragon's Tears to unlock a different ending when you beat the final boss.
    16. Now, back to the Fountainhead Palace.

    Vermilion Bridge

    Video Guide: Fountainhead Palace and Mibu Manor

    1. Now that you've defeated the True Monk once and for all, the path to the rest of the palace lies before you. Take in the amazing vista of the large palace area, the giant lake, the many buildings along the side and front, and the large falls closest to you.
    2. Grapple down along the right side of the ridge to get down the falls area. There are a few paths to take among these buildings here, but for now the most straightforward will do.
    3. At the entrance to the first building you'll find a Mibu Balloon of Soul past a tree with some Spirit Emblems. Inside the building is another Mist Noble - like the one you found in the Hidden Forest. However, this enemy isn't as big of a pushover as his friend. Just by looking at you, he'll start sucking your lifeforce - causing massive terror buildup frighteningly fast.
    4. Unlike normal terror buildup - you won't die outright, but you'll become enfeebled: losing almost all of your maximum health and resurrection nodes - and you can only stagger about with slow swings - as the Mist Noble will then try to lurch forward to grab and beat you do death. You can't use much of any items either in this state - the only way to get back to normal is to either kill the Mist Noble, or get far enough away that it loses interest. It's highly recommended you only approach these enemies from behind, or sprint in for a kill before they can start sucking you dry.
    5. Past this enemy, you'll find another Mist Noble in the next room overlooking a balcony - but also watch out for an additional Mist Noble behind some screens over by the other exit that looks out across the lake.
    6. Whatever you do, DO NOT try and swim out onto the lake. An imposing enemy is perched high on the tree on the other side, and will hurl lightning blasts toward you that will electrify the water around the blast to kill you quickly. She will only do this when you head into deep water anywhere in this region. For now - stick to the cover of the buildings.
    7. To get to the next building, you can either look for a broken wall to pass through, or go by ways of the falls and grab a Light Coin Purse on a broken pillar. If you take the back path, you'll spot several enemies patrolling here - these are the Okami Warrior Women spoken of in legend, and they are formidable opponents indeed.
    8. These long-necked creatures are experts with swords and bows alike - and will often prance about in unpredictable patterns before leaping in with jabs or spinning slashes. If they give out a strange battle cry, beware of a multi-hit combo - but deflecting them can break their posture fast. The warriors can utilize a long range jumping thrust you can Mikiri Counter, but may also jump up for a downward stab or strike.
    9. You've actually heard about these creatures before - in the text of the Sabimaru, which was used to great effect against these god-like enemies. The legends hold true, as the Okami are very weak to poison, and even a single hit of the Sabimaru will cause them to retch from the poison - so be sure to use it when you need to get some breathing room.
    10. Note that the two patrolling sword users are watched over by an archer on the lower roof. In order to get to him unannounced, you can take the path through the broken wall and look for grapple points - but you can also get to the top of the roof, where three more archers watch a lone sword user practicing on a bridge overlooking the water.
    11. Take out the trio - they go down much faster than the warriors, but can fire off volleys in quick succession - and then ambush the practicing warrior and grab the Lump of Grave Wax. If
    12. you're feeling gutsy, you can also try jumping over the deep waters to a broken building to the right (the covering will stop you from getting hit by lightning) and grab the Adamantite Scrap inside.
    13. Back on the other side of the building closest to the falls, kill the archer on the second level before dropping down to battle the Okami warriors, and then look inside the building for a Mibu Balloon of Wealth - but be sure not to get your soul sucked by the Mist Noble hiding in the alcove to the left.
    14. When you reach the next area, you'll find a large enclosed courtyard, with a broken wall entrance to the far right, or you can hop onto the buildings to climb over the wall and find a Ceramic Shard in a nest.
    15. In this next area, several dog-like beasts are wandering the shallows. If it looks like a dog, acts like a dog, and sounds...odd - it can probably die just as easily. A shuriken or two will put one down, as does a single deflection - or you can have fun with the Finger Whistle to make them go mad. Beware - sometimes the dog creatures will jump into the air and emit a lightning bolt that can deal a lot of damage if you aren't prepared. Consider investing in Eel Livers, or try jumping to the side to avoid them - but remember the ability to disperse electricity by slashing while still in the air.
    16. Check the middle clearing where the group of creatures were to find Ungo's Sugar and a Heavy Coin Purse, then look for an entrance to the large building on the side. Three of the dog creatures are in here - as is a Mist Noble hiding off on the left corner. You can use the Finger Whistle to turn the tables on them, and then ambush and kill the survivors. Grab the Adamantite Scrap, and then head outside.
    17. If you climb to the roof of the building, look along the backside and you'll find an Okami Warrior and Okami Archer sitting along the roof in wait - so engage carefully. Down below is a group of three dog creatures, and three more in the small building nearby - use the Finger Whistle (without targeting specifically) to let them tear each other to pieces before grabbing the Eel Liver they were guarding.
    18. Finally, there's some more beasts looking around a tree by the large gate at the end. Dispose of them to find Yashariku's Sugar, and the path to the next area.

    Mibu Manor

    1. Entering the small courtyard, commune with the Mibu Manor Sculptor's Idol. The front door appears locked, so instead take a right through a broken wall to find a small stairs leading inside.
    2. Another Mist Noble patrols these halls - wait for him to pass and then stab him in the back, and then crawl along the passage to avoid the gaze of another Mist Noble across a small pool. In the next room, a woman will call to you. She actually seems pretty decent, and warns you of the many Mist Nobles here - especially in the courtyard ahead.
    3. Speak to her again, and she'll ask that you help her father - who is entranced by the Great Carp, and begs you to help him in some way.
    4. Heed the woman's warning, and use the set of screens on the left side of the room to sneak past the courtyard brimming with Mist Nobles. Hug the left wall and grab the Bite Down item -
    5. this can be useful if you need to dart past a group of them, die, and then resurrect when they look the other way.
    6. When you reach the end of the room, it looks out on another small courtyard with a tree surrounded by tall grass, and a bridge where a Mist Noble plays his flute, and alternates looking toward you and away from you. When he looks away, run out and dart to the left to kill the Mist Noble that was watching your entry earlier, and run into the pond to grab the Ako's Sugar before the one on the bridge turns back. Wait for him to turn again, then race into the tall grass below him, and sneak attack him when the opportunity presents itself.
    7. Next, look off to the left for a building where an Okami Warrior kneels to engage her in combat and lure her away from the Eel Liver she guards. Across from her in an alcove is a Mist Noble - throw a Ceramic Shard to lure him out and gut him when he gets in range. Round the corner and you'll also find Yellow Gunpowder at a dead end. You can almost make out something large just beyond the screen windows - but you can't get out there just yet.
    8. On the other side of the bridge where you killed the Mist Noble, another followed by two Okami Warrior bodyguards will start patrolling the halls in a circle - but note the crawlspace above you can grapple up to.
    9. From here you can watch them pass and ambush them - or use the Finger Whistle to cause some havoc first. Kill the group, and then look in the far back left side of the corridors to find Divine Confetti, and then return to the large room around where the group patrolled to find a single Mist Noble you can drop down behind and kill, and then loot the Lump of Grave Wax in a small covered area.
    10. The Divine Noble here was facing the large courtyard - but there are still three left, so skirt them and keep to the outer left side. It's tempting to creep up on the left-most Divine Noble - but he's being watched by a large crowd in the building next to him. Instead toss a Ceramic Shard at him from around a corner to silence him, and then rush the two Mist Nobles on the bridges before the Okami Warriors see what's going on.
    11. Two Warriors will emerge from the hut where a Mist Noble with a red robe sits (oddly enough this one won't try to cause terror damage), and one Warrior has a naginata spear. Like the Corrupted Monk, this Okami Warrior may try a spinning low sweep attack that can go on for three rotations - or jump into the air for a flying slam attack. Even the Sabimaru can't poison these warriors right away - and the Okami Warriors wearing purple can also leap into the air to charge up their spears with lightning to send your way - but you can reflect it back at them to stun them if you're quick. Be sure to lead them on a merry chase around the manor to divide and conquer.
    12. When you return to the main courtyard, grab the Pellet by the tree, and head in the building to find Adamantite Scrap, as well as a chest holding Divine Grass. On the other side of the building, the last group awaits - an Okami Warrior with a naginata and a Divine Noble. Grab the Okami's attention to lure it away from the prying eyes of the Mist Noble, and then come back to rush the noble - but watch out for another Mist Noble hiding to the left waiting to catch you off guard.
    13. Before leaving, turn around from where the duo was to look along the wooden floors, and you'll find a broken part of the floor leading to an underwater passage. In this hidden room, you'll find a treasure trove: Three Treasure Carp Scale, and a chest holding Water of the Palace.
    14. If you remember back - there is someone who was craving these waters. Return to the Mibu Village in the Ashina Depths and travel to the Head Priest's house. Enter via the secret revolving floor under the building to find him guzzling the water until there's none left - and offer him the Water of the Palace. In thanks, he'll give you Dragonspring Sake, and if you return later, you'll find him transformed into a Mist Noble (without the power to suck your life force), and you can defeat him to earn a whopping five Treasure Carp Scale!
    15. There's a large series of buildings and platforms before you upon leaving the Mibu Manor - but there's a bit more to see nearby first. Take a left to find a large pagoda with some Divine Confetti around the back, and then look around the other side for the long path going behind the manor itself.

    Mini-Boss - Sakura Bull of the Palace

    Video Guide: Sakura Bull of the Palace and Flower Viewing Stage

    1. As you reach the end of the path, a large variant of the Blazing Bull will appear at the other end. It turns out that even the divine monsters here seem to love lighting a bull's horns on fire and letting it go wild.
    2. It's important to note that this fight is only made harder by several Okami Warriors and Archers crowded around the rooftops of the nearby Mibu Manor. You can turn the tables on them by looking for a grapple point past the Scultpor's Idol, and taking the fight to them. While there's not much else to see along the rooftops of the manor - you can theoretically use the rooftops to get the literal drop on the Sakura Bull and take it out without any fuss at all - since it only has the one health bar.
    3. If you choose to engage it head-to-head, you'll find it behaves much like the Blazing Bull, and drawing it back to its initial pen can help as the beast struggles to turn quickly. Still, it's magical blazing horns can deal piercing damage even when you block - so watch out for the dip in its horns signaling an upwards head thrusting attack, or its violent swiveling of the head from side to side.
    4. You can also still use Shinobi Firecrackers to great effect here - or use the nearby tree branches to grapple above and behind the beast to attack from its rear. Keep up the damage while strafing behind the large beast, and at low health and posture it will eventually buck around until it slams into a wall to topple over.
    5. Defeating this enemy will earn you another Prayer Bead, along with A Beast's Karma Skill that further increases your max Spirit Emblem count by 1. Be sure to also search its pen for three items: a Bulging Coin Purse, Yashariku's Sugar, and Adamantite Scrap.

    Flower Viewing Stage

    1. Back at the Sculptor's Idol, it's time to move forward. As you approach the bridge to the Flower Viewing Stage, a giant carp will leap out of the water and tear the bridge to pieces before diving away. Neat.
    2. Be sure to look for a grapple point to the side - falling into the deep water will trigger the Okami lightning warrior in the distance to hurl more bolts - so steer clear of the depths. Across the bridge, you can spot two Okami Warriors practicing... kickball?
    3. It's no joke. The Okami Kickballers will throw their balls high into the air before slamming them at you - some will arc around to hit you from the side as a distraction, while others they can slam into you with incredible speed and power. However, you may not want to engage the two on the platform just yet - two more Kickballers are waiting on the platform above to pelt you, so let's find a different path.
    4. Go down to the building on the left where the other side of the bridge was to find a Mibu Balloon of Soul, and then go around the left side of the building to find a path up the falls where a lone Okami Kickballer waits - and ambush her before she can start pelting you. Even at close range, they can lash out with a few kicks that deal surprising damage - but if you wait at range for them to leap into the air, you can rush in and jump up next to them to perform an Anti-Air Deathblow to silence them quickly.
    5. You can't get to the house at the top of the falls off to the left just yet, but be sure to climb onto the roof of the building you passed to eliminate another Okami Warrior - then look for the two Kickballers on top of the balcony nearby. Rush them quickly before they can put up a defense, and try not to raise the attention of those below you.
    6. There's also two more Okami Warriors behind you along the falls near a broken bridge - they can also be snuck up on if you grapple up to the broken bridge and get in behind them. With the enemies on the upper platforms gone, you can now drop down to the lower level to find two more Okami Warriors patrolling the underside of the platforms, and then finally take out the two on the main platform without getting ambushed.
    7. When you've cleared this first main platform, be sure to check the shallows under the platform to find a Mibu Balloon of Soul among the rocks leading out to the deeper water, and then head back towards the falls to find various bridges leading to the next central platform. The highest bridge lets you grapple onto a thin roof area, and if you check the tall tower near you, look for Gokan's Sugar in a nest at the top.
    8. Climbing to the upper platforms and looking further ahead, you can spot two Okami Warriors near the center platform, another patrolling between them and the last two Okami Warriors at the back of the platform by the waterfalls and an old shrine.
    9. Engaging this group can be tricky - so be sure to use everything in your arsenal like the Sabimaru or Finger Whistle to distract and disorient - and try taking out the enemies in the back before working your way forward like you did before. To do this, look for a lower path leading to a bridge along the back that is broken, and you can drop down to find more of the dog-like creatures below guarding an Eel Liver, as well as a Ceramic Shard further downstream.
    10. Looking up from the waterways, you should be able to spot the two Okami Warriors armed with naginatas with their backs turned to you - making it an ideal strike point. Better yet, use the Puppeteer Ninjutsu to turn one to your side and let him rip into the others before finishing up the job - just watch out for stray lightning bolts.
    11. You're getting closer to the large tree where that lightning-hurling jerk is - but not close enough. Since you can't get up from here, return upstream to the falls and go behind the small shrine to find a waterfall surrounded by dog-like creatures - and engage them quickly with shurikens to find two Mibu Possession Balloons. If you hear a strange noise nearby - that's because on the falls further to the right is a Shichimen Warrior practicing his magic, but we'll deal with him
    12. later. For now, return to the small shrine hut and grapple atop it to find more platforms against the cliff that the waterfalls are pouring down.
    13. Move up these rock platforms and across branches sticking out of the waterfalls to reach to the top of the palace stairs - only to find the door here locked, and what sounds like many Mist Nobles on the other side.
    14. Your only way forward seems to lie with the large lake - so getting rid of that enemy on the tree is our goal. Head down the stairs from the locked door to a basin with some Spirit Emblems. The path to the left has broken, and the right leads to a longer bridge with a Pellet before it too ends with a broken edge.
    15. Moving straight ahead, watch for an Okami Warrior and Archer. Advance carefully, and use the Loaded Umbrella if you need to power through the arrows to reach the duo and then eliminate them.
    16. On the other side of this path is a viewing platform that looks down upon another central platform where a total of five Okami Warriors are sitting - not great odds. You'll need to approach this area extremely carefully - but there's somewhere else we can go first.

    Near Pot Noble

    1. Before heading down, be sure to turn around and head back to the basin - but look up for a grapple point to reach the roof of the bridge.
    2. Move left along the bridge to wrap around to the other side of a tall roof, and you can spot a cave entrance just above you along the waterfalls that you can grapple to. Take out the few Crawling Geckos in the small tunnel, and you'll exit out above the large building we couldn't reach on the far side of this area!
    3. Drop down, and look around the front of the building to grab Divine Confetti. Off to the side, you can also find... another Pot Noble! Be sure to commune with the Near Pot Noble Sculptor's Idol, and then speak to Pot Noble Koremori, who has an entirely different selection of wares than Pot Noble Harunaga in the Hirata Estate.
    4. These items include a Dragon's Blood Droplet, and two Lapis Lazuli that can be used as crafting materials to make some of the best Prosthetic Tool Upgrades you'll ever get. He also sells the final Mask Fragment. When combined with Pot Noble Harunaga and the Dungeon Memorial Mob's Mask Fragments - you can form the Dancing Dragon Mask, which can convert 5 extra Skill Points into 1 Attack Power, which is not so useful now as it will be for New Game Plus.
    5. Now return to the last central platform where the five Okami Warriors are. The one dancing in the middle with the purple outfit is the most dangerous, as only they are capable of lightning attacks, which she will leap into the air and charge her sword to slice down - much like Genichiro.
    6. You may want to again use the Puppeteer Ninjutsu to turn one of the Okami Warriors to your side, let them get all the attention, and then attack the stragglers from behind as they surround your puppet. If you see the main Okami leap into the air - be prepared to jump as well, as you can reflect the lightning into all of your enemies and stun an entire group at once, and finish them while they remain stunned.
    7. Clear out the group, and you'll be rewarded with some Dragonspring Sake to share with who you please.

    Mini-Boss - Okami Leader Shizu

    1. Be sure to commune with the Great Sakura Sculptor's Idol, and then grapple to the large branch to deal with the Okami Leader.
    2. As you have probably guessed from earlier, this perilous kickballer will send electrified projectiles straight at you and deal a ton of damage if you are grounded - which means you'll either have to be great at dodging, or great at timing to disperse the electricity.
    3. Just in case, down an Eel Liver to make sure you won't get one-hit killed, and then start advancing. Note that in addition to dodging or dispersing attacks, there are a few grapple points along the branch you can use - jump out of harm's way to the side and off the branch entirely, and then grapple back to resume your approach.
    4. Even when you close the distance, she can still be a pain to deal with even doing regular kicks and kickball attacks - so its actually better to wait at a close distance until you see her start sparking up and launching a kickball in the air. When his happens, sprint forward and jump into her to get the prompt for an Anti-Air Deathblow, which will end the fight in an instant.
    5. Defeating the Okami Leader will not only grant you another Prayer Bead, but now the entire region is free from her tyranny - and the waters are open to you.

    Great Sakura

    Video Guide: Great Sakura and Underwater Path to the Palace

    1. Resting back up at the Great Sakura Sculptor's Idol, there's a lot to explore and do now. First thing's first - return to the tree branch where you killed the Okami Leader, and look down and to the left for a big low branch holding a Bulging Coin Purse.
    2. Head back up to the base of the upper tree, and drop down around the other side to find several dog-creatures guarding a Light Coin Purse, and then search around the sides to find a Heavy Coin Purse in a nest.
    3. From here, you can see paths leading back under the main platforms all the way to where you first entered - as well as a stream guarded by more dog-creatures that leads up to the Shichimen Warrior. Off to the right is another lower main platform that's separated by a large stream emptying into the lake. Since we can't quite reach it yet, take the path upstream.

    Mini-Boss - Shichimen Warrior

    1. Much like your previous encounters with this foe, having the Phoenix's Lilac Umbrella will be a game changer. Since the Shichimen Warrior is located at the far end of a stream by the falls, he'll make you run towards him, all while shooting out concentrated soul beams that can terrify you extremely fast.
    2. In order to get past his salvos, unload your umbrella and turtle up to his face - and be sure any other dog-creatures along the rocks or streams are already killed, as you don't want stray lightning bolts hitting you during with fight.
    3. The Shichimen Warrior will always alternate using jumping away while spreading soul orbs across the streambed, or trying to wack you with his staff once or twice. He can also send forth a stream of orbs that are only really dangerous at range - otherwise you can strafe behind him, and the same can be said for his soul stream.
    4. If he ever actually blocks your attacks, he'll follow this by disappearing to reappear at the other end of the stream, and continue firing off soul streams - which he'll also do when you've taken off one of his health bars. Just remain calm and turtle up when you need to get close to him, and don't be afraid to apply your shield and walk into floating orbs to clear the field so you can attack him for longer intervals before dodging away from them.
    5. Keep at it much like how you dealt with his brothers, and be ready to sprint downstream a moment's notice until you finally get rid of his health. Killing him will drop another Lapis Lazuli. Be sure to also inspect the waterfall he was standing in and you'll find a Bundled Jizo Statue.

    The Lake

    1. Since you can't get over to the last platform area, it's time to head into the water. Be sure to look below the platform you can't reach just yet, and you'll find a Treasure Carp to kill, and Precious Bait below it.
    2. As you swim forward, you'll spot a huge canyon going down below you, and half-submerged building that the Giant Carp swims past on the left. Be sure to check inside the drowned building to find a Lump of Grave Wax, and check the roof for an Old Woman with a clue - there's a path to the palace through the cave where the Great Carp makes its home, and she also wants you to open the doors for her.
    3. There's also a few more underwater buildings around the cave entrance, and you can find 3 Treasure Carp Scale in one building, and two more Treasure Carps nearby heading toward where you first started this area.
    4. Before entering the cave or canyon, be sure around the canyon going by the broken bridge on the far side to find two more Treasure Carps, and then swim towards the Mibu Manor to find a partially submerged hall with a nest on the roof you can grab Adamantite Scrap.
    5. Avoid the Mist Noble watching from the manor, and swim towards the Flower Viewing Platform Sculptor's Idol on the far side of the lake.
    6. Below the broken bridge you'll find a host of man-eating fish, and along the floor is a Red Lump you can collect. Further back behind the broken bridge, look carefully among the foliage along the ground for Scrap Magnetite and a Light Coin Purse.
    7. Now head back to swim down into the large canyon in the middle of the lake. Circling the canyon above are two groups of man-eating fish guarding one Treasure Carp apiece. Kill the guards and wait for the Treasure Carp to spawn back before ambushing them.
    8. When you start swimming down you'll notice a few giant underwater buildings. Look below the destroyed interior of one of the bigger buildings and you'll find some Precious Bait, while the longer building on the opposite side has a Light Coin Purse inside.

    Mini-Boss - Underwater Headless

    1. Swimming deeper into the canyon, you'll soon spot a Headless - but he's not alone - there's a phantom version of the Headless not far below, and will mimic the main Headless attacks with
    2. blasts from his sword and the spooky hair tendrils. You don't want to focus on the ghostly purple double at all, as he does nothing except waste your time.
    3. Instead, lock onto the main Headless and keep strafe swimming in circles before darting in for a quick thrust, and then swimming past and away before he can retaliate. You'll need to time it so that you've just evaded a sword slash or his hair tendrils, and try to keep the phantom in frame to make sure you don't get hit swimming in.
    4. Dart in and out and be careful with your attacks - there's no rush here, posture damage is irrelevant and each lunging strike takes off a good chunk of health. Keep at it and when you finally go in for a deathblow, the phantom will disappear too. Killing this Headless grants you Yashariku's Spiritfall, an item that lets you use the effects of Yashariku's Sugar as much as you want, as long as you have the spirit emblems to spare.
    5. Once the pair are removed from the battle, search up behind where the Headless was for some Precious Bait on a log, and search the lowest pit for a large Giant Carp skeleton covered in glow worms, and nearby you can find 2 Treasure Carp Scale, and a chest holding a Prayer Bead.

    Path to the Palace Grounds

    1. Now it's time to enter the Great Carp's lair. Swim over to the far side of the lake and look for a sloping underwater path where you last saw the Great Carp, and you'll find a Ceramic Shard at the opening to the cave.
    2. Swimming slowly inside, duck into the tall reeds as the Great Carp swims below you into the large cave. If he sees you, he'll slam into you and likely kill you or severely wound you - so you want to avoid his gaze much like the Great Serpent.
    3. There are ruined buildings on each side of the cave, and you can use them to stay out of his line of sight. If he sees you, he'll charge in and destroy the entire building section - but often slam his head into the wall in the process, and get dazed for a few moments, giving you time to get out of there and hide again.
    4. It's recommended you take to the right wall when you see him swim up and to the left, and then slowly move through the different building sections as slowly tilt upwards. Once you reach the end, look up and you'll see a large building embedded in the rocks with a hole in the wall near the top - that's your exit. Wait for the Great Carp to swim back to the entrance, and then dart up towards the far wall.
    5. Don't worry if he sees you enter, much like the Great Serpent he won't be able to follow you into the small tunnel, and will slam into the large building to no effect. Once you reach the other side, swim up and out of a pool where a waterfall is emptying out.
    6. Head up the stream and start grappling between trees to reach a high ledge and here you'll find the Palace Grounds Sculptor's Idol.
    7. Head inside to discover... quite a scene. Several red-robed Mist Nobles are in the darkened palace feasting on the bodies of Okami Warriors, all over the place. As you move through the grisly scene, remember to open the door on the left where the main stairway was to open up a shortcut to bypass the Great Carp's cave.
    8. From here, move up the path until one of the Mist Nobles finally looks up to spot you, alerting his friends. Kill them or just move on (these ones can't suck your soul out), and look for a chest at the end of the room that holds the very last Gourd Seed - which should bring your maximum charges up to 10.

    Feeding Grounds

    Video Guide: Feeding Grounds and Great Carp Quest

    1. As you leave the palace behind, know that if you rest, and have spoken with the old woman who asked that you open the door, you can return to find her getting her revenge on the Mist Nobles before dying herself.
    2. Leave the palace, and look back towards the cliff with the Sculptor's Idol to find a grapple point up to the roof. From here you can cross back to look down at the stairs to the main door - but take a look up to the left instead. This unique vantage points offers a grapple point to a large tree branch, and then down to the platforms you couldn't reach before.
    3. As you grapple across to the first roof, watch for a nearby Okami Archer on another rooftop that will start taking potshots at you. Quickly grapple to her and take her out, and then move along the rooftops to the far back building. Drop down and you'll find Ako's Sugar along the back of the building, and then head down to the broken bridge to find an electric-attacking Okami Warrior guarding a Heavy Coin Purse.
    4. Now hop over to the middle platform, and look at the back to find Ungo's Sugar along the path leading down to the Feeding Grounds Sculptor's Idol. Here you will find the large man who is apparently the father of the old women, including the one in the Mibu Manor. His life is now devoted to feeding the Great Carp, which you can do by ringing the nearby bell to summon him.
    5. When the Great Carp arrives, you can feed it the Precious Bait you've been finding, and then talk to the man to get rewards for each bait you give. One Bait nets you one Treasure Carp Scale, two gets you three Treasure Carp Scale, but after that the large man won't dish out any more rewards - claiming moderation is key.
    6. It's about this time that you should pay the Pot Nobles a visit. If you've bought a few things from Pot Noble Koremori, spending at least 7 scales, he'll mention that you should beware of another Pot Noble seeking to kill the Great Carp and take his place.
    7. However, after spending a few more scales, Pot Noble Koremori will give you the Truly Precious Bait, and ask you to secretly feed it to the Great Carp.
    8. This sounds pretty suspicious, and if you return to Pot Noble Harunaga back at the Hirata Estate, he'll also give you a similar story - asking for you to give his specific Truly Precious Bait to the Great Carp without telling the others.
    9. Both offer the same reward, and while their motives seem highly suspect, it may be what's needed to end the old woman's father from his eternal fate of caring for the master. Speaking of which - you can fight the giant father if you wish, he fights exactly like a Chained Ogre - complete with two health bars and grabs, but offers nothing upon his death.
    10. However, if you return to ring the bell and select the Truly Precious Bait for either Pot Nobles, a few things will happen. The Great Carp will immediately retreat after eating the bait, and after
    11. resting, you'll find the father in great distress at the Great Carp's disappearance, but where did he go?
    12. You can check the cave and the carp will be gone, which means you can now swim down to loot a Light Coin Purse and Scrap Magnetite near the bottom of the cave.
    13. Dead things tend to float downstream, and we know that the waters of the Fountainhead pool in the Sunken Valley.
    14. If you return to the Guardian Ape's Watering Hole, you'll find the giant corpse of the Great Carp - and in front of him you can loot the Great White Whisker. Return to the Feeding Platform and offer it to the father to relieve him of his duty, and he'll reward you with a Divine Grass. Rest, and you'll find him at peace with his remaining daughter beside him as she too can finally rest.
    15. As for the Pot Nobles - whichever Pot Noble you didn't side with will now be found dead in their toppled pot. If you helped Pot Noble Harunaga, he'll now take the form of a large red-eyed Carp near his pot, while Pot Noble Koremori is located below where his pot was - and the pot of the survivor now holds the treasure, a Lapis Lazuli. This, the one from the Schichimen Warrior, plus the two sold by the Pot Nobles are all you can get for now. A new boss that appears after finishing Fountainhead Palace drops the final 2 for a total of 6 per playthrough - use them wisely! As for the new red-eyed carp, they'll still sell you the rest of their inventory, and expand it with whatever their competitor had in their stock before death.

    Sanctuary

    1. As you move out of the Palace Grounds building the Mist Nobles were in, you'll find a large grove with several trees, and Crawling Geckos crawling about the ground by twin waterfalls - and you can find a Mibu Balloon of Soul by one of the waterfalls.
    2. Grapple up the trees to a sloping stairway above, and move with caution. In the next clearing are two Okami Warriors with the power of lightning - a sword user and naginata wielder. In addition to this, a third Okami Kickballer is a bit higher to the right - waiting to ambush you form behind when you reach the clearing.
    3. Facing all three at once is a very risky proposition - as all three can use lightning attacks that can be hard to disperse when coming from all directions. Instead of heading up into the clearing, look along the right of the path for a path to grapple up to alert only the Kickballer - and grapple up to stop her before she can start engaging in lightning attacks (remember that you can do an anti-air deathblow if you jump up while she's charging her attack in the air).
    4. With the kickballer out of the way, be sure to use an Eel Liver and take on the other two Okami Warriors. Try to angle yourself on the stairs above them, as lightning blasts are harder to dodge and even harder to disperse back if your lower than them and don't have room before falling to the ground.
    5. You'll want to fight aggressively, use the Sabimaru, and keep attacking to prevent them from jumping into the air - either chasing them down with slashes or using Shurikens to keep them in check. Remember that they have a number of other attacks - including perilous jumping thrust attacks and low sweeping swings you need to jump over.
    6. Once the trio has been removed from play, continue up the stairway and note a Dragon's Blood Droplet on the left before you reach the Sanctuary Sculptor's Idol. At the top of the path, as lightning strikes around you, you'll find a small cave at the top of the fountainhead - inside is the
    7. body lying against a large stone - Lord Takeru perhaps? Kneel beside the body and pray at the stone to transport yourself to the Divine Realm.
    8. Note: If you are still trying to complete the side quests that allow you to unlock the two alternate endings instead of the Immortal Severance - DO NOT DEFEAT THIS BOSS. Once the boss is defeated, certain events will be set in motion that lock you on a collision course with the ending, and you will not be able to deviate when the time comes. See our How to Get All Endings for more details.

    Boss Battle - Old Dragons of the Tree

    Video Guide: Old Dragons of the Tree and the Divine Dragon

    1. Praying at the stone in the cave will take you to the mystical Divine Realm, shrouded in fog among the clouds high over Ashina - and before you is a large withered tree. As the petals fall into the fog, an eerie a sickly looking dragon will emerge. These are the Old Dragons of the Tree, and they've seen better days.
    2. As the relatively small dragon approaches you, it will be joined by even more old dragons that pop up all around you - each of them lends their health to the overall health bar for this phase of the fight. In truth, the Old Dragons aren't all that dangerous. They only have two attacks: very slow smacks with the flute-like instruments they possess, or retching and coughing up clouds of poison, which is the real thing you need to watch out for.
    3. In order to avoid getting poisoned and overwhelmed, you should try to strafe around the group and gets some slashes in around the edges of the group - away from the poison clouds. You can also simply use Contact Medicine to give yourself a very low poison effect that can't be overridden by their more powerful poison - allowing you to wade in and start dishing out damage. Using sweeping attacks - like the Whirlwind Slash or Mortal Draw - you can attack multiple dragons at once, and you'll find they don't have a lot of health.
    4. After a bit of this, they'll all start bobbing up and down and unison before raising themselves up to the sky, as giant roots bursts forth from the ground to try and hit you as they burst out - three in a row. Sprint away from them, and then run back to the remaining root and grapple up top.
    5. From your new vantage point, you'll find that you can instantly deathblow any Dragon you jump down on, and unique to a Plunging Deathblow is the ability to use them as an extension of your blade to swing the dying dragon into his friends and instantly kill any nearby dragons. This is by far your best tactic for clearing out swaths of Old Dragons at a time, and can make this fight go by very fast.
    6. As you near the end of this section, several healthier reddish dragons will emerge along with the gray ones. These are not part of the overall bosses health, and should be ignored entirely. It's better just to run around them until they bring the roots back up - and lure the grey dragons close to pounce on them once more. Once all the Old Dragons have been defeated, the tree in the distance will spring to life, heralding the appearance of the Divine Dragon.

    Boss Battle - Divine Dragon

    1. This fight will play out in a very strange way - and starts by the Divine Dragon pushing you into the air and far back into the arena. The Divine Dragon is impossibly large, and holds a great lightning blade with its one large arm.
    2. After pushing you backwards, five large roots will burst forth around the Divine Dragon, which serves as your main points in this fight. The Divine Dragon itself only has a few real attacks - several low sweep attacks you can avoid by grappling to the roots, and many overhead vertical slash attacks that can be blocked, or simply avoided by running or grappling from side to side.
    3. Since the Divine Dragon is so far up, there's only one way to really damage this boss - Lightning Reversal. Look along the root platforms to see a few sparkling with electricity. When you grapple to one of these roots, you'll be shot up into the air and struck by lightning. Thankfully, you'll be high up into the air and have plenty of time to strike your lightning blast at the Divine Dragon.
    4. This will deal a large amount of damage - so long as you make sure to target the Divine Dragon and that its not moving low or away to avoid the blast. Sometimes you'll get hit with another gust of wind, and need to run back to the roots while dodging incoming sword blasts.
    5. As long as you keep grappling between roots to unleash lightning reversals, eventually the Divine Dragon's health will get low, and the roots will collapse as you are sent backwards one last time. At this point, you'll need to survive a gauntlet of slashes from the Divine Dragon's blade.
    6. Block the incoming vertical slashes and the shockwaves that come, and wait for the dragon to twirl its blade in a circle for a quick slash followed by a small low sweep, another slash and a bigger low sweep. Once this combo attack concludes, the roots will rise up once more.
    7. Run up to the nearest root to get blasted higher and higher still into the air before being struck, and unleash the mother of all lightning blasts into the Divine Dragon's face. This will cause it to crash to the ground, resting its head by the sword, and giving you a spot to run up to its eye and carve him a new tear duct, and you can finally obtain the Divine Dragon's Tears, as well as the Memory: Divine Dragon..
    8. With your objective complete, you'll be automatically expelled from the Divine Realm back to Ashina Castle - but something has gone horribly wrong in your absence.
    ❯
    Chapter XII

    Ashina Castle (Night)

    7 areas7 bosses
    0/0
    Objectives 14
    1. Lord Kuro's Room
    2. Mini-Boss - Ashina Elite Ujinari Mizuo
    3. Upper Tower - Antechamber
    4. Ashina Castle Grounds
    5. Path to the Old Grave
    6. Mini-Boss - Shigekichi of the Red Guard
    7. Mini-Boss - Headless
    8. Ashina Outskirts - Stairway
    9. Boss Fight - Demon of Hatred
    10. Mini-Boss - Seven Ashina Spears - Shume Masaji Oniwa
    11. Near Secret Passage
    12. Boss Fight - Genichiro - Way of Tomoe
    13. Boss Fight - Sword Saint Isshin
    14. Ending
    Walkthrough

    Lord Kuro's Room

    Video Guide: Ashina Castle (Night) Castle Grounds

    1. Returning to Lord Kuro's Room, you'll find the young lord has disappeared, and in his place is Emma and a fallen Isshin Ashina. Speak to her to learn what's going on, and Emma will give you the Secret Passage Key, which can unlock the door back to where you first tried to escape with Lord Kuro and were cut down by Genichiro.
    2. Things will be very different in Ashina now - far more than the changes that happened at dusk. Even worse, if you take the Shinobi Shortcut door to the Dilapidated Temple, you'll find that the Sculptor has left for mysterious reasons, and the branch grapple points leading to the Outskirts have been obliterated. Thankfully you can still upgrade your own Shinobi Prosthetic on your own - and you should absolutely be sure to get one of the Lazulite upgrades, as well as Suzaku's Lotus Umbrella - for reasons we'll explain soon.
    3. The Abandoned Dungeon Entrance has also been sealed - meaning the only way to progress is from Lord Kuro's room, as the Upper Tower has also been closed. If you haven't already, make sure to turn in your last Gourd Seed to Emma, as its all she can do now.
    4. Exiting Kuro's Room from the window, you can look down below to see a battle raging on the stairway leading to the Ashina Castle as Japan's Central Forces lay siege to what's left of Ashina's troops with some serious firepower. All of the Lone Shadows are gone from the rooftops - so you could in theory move down the rooftops towards the Reservoir to see this finished quickly - but there's more to do here if you want to face some more tough enemies for more loot.

    Mini-Boss - Ashina Elite Ujinari Mizuo

    1. If you move along the rooftops down to Isshin's old room, you can head downstairs to find an Ashina Elite has taken up residence in the room below. Ashina Elite Ujinari Mizuo appears to have taken drastic measures to defend his lord, and has ingested a Red Lump to gain red-eyes.
    2. Because of this, this mini-boss can deal incredible damage, even more so than usual considering his Iaido stance of unsheathing his blade for fast strikes, and takes longer to kill. He'll also change up his fast two-hit unsheathing strike with a quick punch, retreating slash, or an unblockable sweep.
    3. However, if you hit him with a Flame Vent - after deflecting his two-hit strike, you can scare him just as you did the Chained Ogre, and can even inflict more damage if you use the Living Force Skill to imbue your blade with fire and lay into him with increased fire attacks. Remember that the fire won't stun him for too long, so get in your hits and then be ready to deflect when he resheathes his blade, and wait for the next opportunity to hit him with more flames.
    4. Defeating him will earn you another Prayer Bead, and you can also find Yashariku's Sugar in the hall leading back to the Antechamber.

    Upper Tower - Antechamber

    1. While the dojo has nothing new (the Vilehand's henchmen is the only person up here), you can head back into the Antechamber to find the usual crew gone, and a Lone Shadow in the first room guarding a Pellet.
    2. The nearby War Room will explode as two Lone Shadows engage two of the Ashina Elite, and one more Lone Shadow guards the path back down to the Antechamber Sculptor's Idol. You can watch the fight play out if you want, or engage yourself and defeat the winners to find some Adamantite Scrap. There's no real point in exiting through the window as there's no more Lone Shadows to do battle with out here, but you can drop down from the Antechamber to the main hall.
    3. Here you'll find two of the Interior Ministry Soldiers, but you can drop down to execute one from above, and then take out his friend. Be ready for a big fight when you leave by way of the main door, as the Central Forces have brought a lot of troops with them.

    Ashina Castle Grounds

    1. As you leave the tower for the stairway, you'll find a battle raging as a few Ashina Soldiers and elites are gunned down by the Interior Ministry Guards. These men are much like the others you've faced - but differ in intensity.
    2. They carry two large katana swords and swing them with incredible power, though much more slowly than the other infiltrators you've faced. Due to their heavy emphasis on offense, they have pretty terrible posture - so be sure to learn the timing of their slow two-blade slices and deflect them to break their posture - Double Ichimonji can actually break some of them instantly. They also have high powered rifles that fire explosive shots, which is why having Suzaku's Lotus Umbrella can be a big help here.
    3. This becomes even more apparent due to the Central Forces Flamethrower among them - he'll slowly walk around with a large cannon and fire a steady stream of fireworks above before lowering it down in the direction of his foes that does constant damage and fire buildup. Luckily these new enemies all carry Ministry Dousing Powder - which is a bit more effective at clearing way burn status than regular Dousing Powder, but using the burn-resisting shield can also help (you'll still take posture damage so be sure to get away or behind them before coming out of your shield to attack).
    4. Once you clear out the forces on the stairway, look at the top to find Adamantite Scrap, Ministry Dousing Powder, and Fulminated Mercury on the landing halfway down. At the bottom, re-commune with the Ashina Castle Sculptor's Idol. To one side, a regular Samurai General is fighting one of the Interior Ministry Guards on the path to the Reservoir and the final boss, but on the other side is a dying Ashina Soldier who calls out to you.
    5. Speak with him, and he'll plead with you to help the failing guards trying to push back the Central Forces that have taken over the Ashina Outskirts - using a bridge from the Old Grave. You'll learn that a Shinobi Kite has been installed up on the rooftops to be used as a shortcut to get the grave.
    6. At this point you can skip ahead to the final boss, but you'll miss out on one more mini-boss, and a tough optional boss - so consider taking this path if you want to max out your health and attack power.

    Path to the Old Grave

    1. The rest of the castle grounds are light on enemies - the next alley has another plain Samurai General fighting an Interior Ministry Flamethrower next to an Ako's Sugar, and several Taro Troops are staggering about and crying - they won't bother you.
    2. Taking to the rooftops is another story - as several Nightjar Ninja are engaged in battle with red- eyed versions of themselves. That can't be good. Luckily you can just grapple past most of them, or watch and see who wins from a distance and take out the winner - and use your Flame Vent on any red-eyed ninjas who come your way.
    3. When you reach the top of the tower on the right side - you can look up to spot the only Shinobi Kite high up that will let you perform a huge grapple boost all the way to the Old Grave. Here, there's a dying Nightjar Ninja on top of the watchtower that asks for your help, and you can also head up the path to re-commune with the Old Grave Sculptor's Idol.
    4. If you happen to explore up past the Old Grave on the path to the Serpent Shrine, you can find a corridor with many wounded Ashina Soldiers and a Fistful of Ash, and you can cross the bridge to eavesdrop on the Shinobi Hunter. Past him, the path to the Serpent Shrine has been retaken by Monkeys armed with swords and guns. At the Sculptor's Idol you'll find the Monkeys killing off the Shinobi Hounds - led by two Monkey Sword Masters. Defeat them both and you'll get a Bundled Jizo Statue.

    Video Guide: Ashina Castle (Night) Ashina Outskirts Stronghold

    1. Back at the Old Grave, take the path down the empty stairways to the lower watchtower, and you'll find a dying Blackhat Badger (if you finished his questline to get him across the valley at Senpou Temple). He'll thank you for all you've done, and after dying you can loot his body for the Mibu Pilgrimage Balloon, which can be used to acquire more money, loot, spirit emblems AND resurrective power for a limited time.
    2. As you reach the newly erected bridge, you'll find the Ashina Soldiers fighting a losing battle against the Central Forces - if they haven't all been demolished already. Get some free hits in where you can before they take notice, and then lure them back across the bridge away from the riflemen - and used the Suzaku's Lotus Umbrella to soak in any incoming shots.
    3. Take on the soldiers carefully and don't get surrounded by their long blades - use your tools and movement to keep them all in front of you or distracted when possible - and in a pinch, you can use the wounded Ashina Soldiers as free backstabs to either get health, empower your blade, or make a cloud of smoke.
    4. Once the bridge is clear, shield up and rush the lone rifleman on the wall and grab the Ministry Dousing Powder below him, and then look to the right for a soldier hiding around the corner, and back down on the left path to find Adamantite Scrap by the old shrine, guarded by another Interior Ministry Guard.

    Mini-Boss - Shigekichi of the Red Guard

    1. If you grapple up onto the wall above the gate leading to the shrine, you'll find an Interior Ministry Soldier hiding among the tall grass. Kill him, and then look beyond into the courtyard to spot a Sumo Wrestler - Shigekichi of the Red Guard. Note that above him is a lookout who will alert him if he sees you coming - but there are a couple ways to deal with this.
    2. For one, you can just run and jump up to him, kill the Lookout and then run away until Shigekichi gives up. Or, if you're up in the tall grass above the courtyard, quickly sneak along the tree branch, and fire off a shuriken at the lookout to silently take him out, and then run along the platform above the mini-boss to drop in and shave off one of his health bars.
    3. In combat, Shigekichi behaves much like other Sumo Wrestler mini-bosses you've encountered, with a few small but important details. For one, you may find that it's hard to deal meaningful damage or build up posture on this foe - that's because unlike the others, this sumo has armor on, but it's ill-fitting. With this in mind, be sure to equip the Loaded Spear, and find a moment to stab and yank him twice (like when he takes a swig of his drink) to break off the armor.
    4. Without his armor on, you'll be able to deal more damage and increase his posture damage faster - which is good because he'll still deal major posture damage with each strike. Be careful and be ready to move out of range of his long sweeps if you need to back off and block to regenerate your own posture.
    5. Another unique talent that Shigekichi has is the ability to breathe fire instead of poison. This can be extremely deadly if he does with after breaking your posture, or you're not ready to react to it - so have Suzaku's Lotus Umbrella ready and waiting to turtle around him and then strike him back with the Projected Force Skill.
    6. Without his armor, his posture will rarely regenerate - and you can take the battle at your own pace and get in hits when it's safe to whittle his health and posture down to the point where you can finish him off. You'll be rewarded with a Prayer Bead.

    Mini-Boss - Headless

    1. It's time to take out the last remaining Headless in Ashina, if you haven't gone back for him already. He lies in the cave beyond the shrine with the warning sign to the side of the courtyard where you fought Shigekiche.
    2. By now you should have the Phoenix's Lilac Umbrella, the Mottled Purple Gourd, plenty of Divine Confetti that you can now buy on demand, and more. You should have also infested in the Malcontent, an upgrade for the Finger Whistle that can be used to stun the Headless and any other apparitions for several moments.
    3. When combined with Divine Confetti, you can deal massive damage in a short amount of time, but you'll need to be ready for the monster to fight back - and either perfectly time those deflects or quickly swap to the umbrella to guard, and then let loose the Projected Force Skill to apply even more damage.
    4. If you keep alternating between the Malcontent to stun the Headless, and the Lilac Umbrella to defend against its follow up attacks, the Headless should give you much less trouble than the others, and you'll get Ako's Spiritfall as a reward which lets you gain the effects of Ako's Sugar by just using Spirit Emblems instead.

    Ashina Outskirts - Stairway

    1. With Shigekichi down, it's time to take on the Interior Ministry stronghold that's been erected among the ruins of the outskirts. If you look into the hallway above the gate at the top of the stairs, you can find Gachiin's Sugar, and in the room on the left are two Senpou Assassins guarding a Heavy Coin Purse.
    2. As you go down the stairs, expect heavy resistance in the form of many riflemen - some that have taken up position in some of the watchtowers built behind the gate. There's Fulminated Mercury behind some boxes you can take cover behind, as two Interior Ministry Guards will head up the stairs flanked by a Flamethrower, and covered by the riflemen behind them.
    3. Instead of a frontal assault, you can try and keep them guessing by moving up the right and hopping over the high ledges and down to the platforms below. If you can do this without alerting the troops on the ground and in the watchtower - you can swing around and grapple up behind the guard on the tower and take him out quietly, as you can only get up to the tower from behind.
    4. Kill the rifleman quickly and grab the Adamantite Scrap, and then look to the right to climb up onto the platform where the Sculptor's Idol is. If you didn't alert the guards, you can drop down to the base of the watchtower to find another rifleman guarding a Pellet - and then you can take out the patrolling guards on the stairs - just be sure to give the flamethrower a wide berth.
    5. Moving up to the sealed gate, you'll find that poor Anayama the Peddler has been killed. Grapple up and over the gate to the other side and you'll find some Gachiin's Sugar, and the path ahead holds dangers.
    6. An Interior Ministry Soldier waits on the other side of the lower gate, but if you take the path on the right through the high grass and ledges, you'll find many Senpou Assassins here waiting to ambush you, as well as another on the main path's ledge.
    7. Take the path slowly to deal with each new ambush as it comes to you, and watch out for Interior Ministry Soldiers coming down the path head to join the fray - and use the ledges above the path to get away if you need to. Be sure to check at the end of the road for Adamantite Scrap before the path heads upwards.
    8. When the road is clear - note that you can grapple up to visit the Crow Memorial Mob if you need to restock - then take a look at the battlefield before you. Three large watchtowers are positioned at varying heights, with many Interior Ministry Guards patrolling between them, and a few soldiers hiding behind the barricades. On the lower end of the slope, you'll find the building at the bottom filled with Senpou Assassins among the bodies of the old woman and her son.
    9. There's also a patrolling Chained Ogre - not sure how they managed that one, but thankfully he has a low radius of detection and only has the one health bar. He also can't immediately break out of his chains, and can only use his dropkicks and low kicks to attack until his health gets low enough that he'll break free.
    10. Start by taking out the watchtowers first and try and move behind the barricades either up from the Crow Memorial Mob to the roof of the nearby house, or go around the house with the Senpou Assassins and hug the left cliff - using the tree branches to come up behind the lowest watchtower and move up to the highest one by continuing to hug the left side.
    11. When it's clear enough to drop down to the pathways, be sure to check the stairs leading to the lowest watchtower to find Ministry Dousing Powder, Fulminated Mercury below the second watchtower, a Pellet in the ruins of the building next to it, and a Lump of Grave Wax below the highest watchtower - not far from an Interior Ministry Flamethrower.
    12. If you've been sneaky up to this point, you may be able to get a Plunging Deathblow on the Chained Ogre, otherwise lure the other guards away from him so you can take him out last with just the two of you.
    13. Finally, head through the burning gate at the top of the path to find a scene of carnage, fire, and claw marks. As you move through the rubble you can listen to a dying soldier's words, and then find a Sculptor's Idol of all things right where the path ends in flames. This Sculptor's Idol is not like others - it will teleport you straight to the boss arena, over at the Ashina Castle Gate, where you face a foe unlike any other.

    Boss Fight - Demon of Hatred

    Video Guide: Demon of Hatred Boss Fight

    1. Arriving at the same field where you once fought Gyoubu Oniwa, instead you'll encounter a giant monster of a demon tearing apart the Interior Ministry's forces - before turning its attention on you.
    2. Note that if you can't tell by the amount of flames engulfing the castle and outskirts - this fight will have to do with fire in a lot of ways, so you'll want to have the Red Gourd and Ministry Dousing Powder ready. Better yet, having Suzaku's Lotus Umbrella can help mitigate a lot of the more fearsome attacks this boss has.
    3. The Demon of Hatred is a large humanoid beast who happens to also be missing his left arm - replaced by a fiery whip-like appendage in its place, and he'll use it to throw down fireballs or slam down with heavy fire damage. Because of his huge size, range, and fiery attacks, this won't be a traditional boss fight. You can't expect to trade blows or deflect all the incoming attacks so easily - and instead you may have to treat this boss more like something out of Dark Souls or Bloodborne.
    4. A lot of this fight needs to be spent sprinting - many of the Demon's moves are based in slow frontal arcs or sweeps, and by constantly strafing until after an attack has launched, you can make sure you're in the right place to get your own hits in - don't worry about posture, this fight is all about getting his health down.
    5. At range, the Demon has several fire-whip attacks with his burning hand - he may employ to sweeping slices in front of him, followed by a long overhead whip strike that covers a lot of vertical distance, but can be avoided by running to the side and then leaping in for attacks when it lands.
    6. If the Demon pulls back and covers his head with his normal arm, be ready for him to sweep his fiery appendage in front of him and let loose a cluster of fire bombs in front of him (which he may do twice), though the Suzaku Umbrella can help negate this.
    7. Another big attack that you can umbrella up for at close range is if you see the Demon jump high into the air - he's going to belly flop down in a giant explosion that you can soak with the umbrella. However at range, you'll have a moment to notice a grapple point appears on the sword stuck in his arm - and you can grapple above the coming shockwave blast that will otherwise knock you flat.
    8. At close range, the Demon still has a few attacks to watch out for - including stomps with his foot (mostly his right foot) that you can deflect, or repeated head slams you can sprint around to his backside to attack him while he's busy putting his face in the ground. If he does a close
    9. fiery hand sweep, he'll follow up with a quick stomp and then an uppercut from his crouched position which is hard to dodge away from - but you can dodge by running close to his legs if you are quick.
    10. After getting one deathblow off on the Demon, he'll kneel to the ground for a moment - but don't be fooled - you can't damage him until he explodes with rage and sends you hurling backwards.
    11. In this phase, he'll start to employ even more fire based attacks which can be extremely deadly. If you see him sway his body to your left with his foot raised, he'll stomp down to let out a deadly flame trail in his path straight at you. It can be hard to avoid - as you'll need to sprint and jump to the left side and then grapple to him to attack back, but if you're too close you may want to consider using your shield instead as it usually takes too long for the grapple point to appear.
    12. He may also start shaking his head wildly, as fireballs appear in front of him and track you down. This is also a deadly move that can be blocked with the shield (though the increasing posture damage can wear on you), but you can also keep sprinting while running right and slowly towards him, then dash straight when he lands in a T pose to attack him.
    13. In his final phase, things get even more heated - literally. He'll usually start with an unblockable whip low sweep that will spin several times, and before igniting the edge of his range in a giant fire wall.
    14. The walls for flame can last annoyingly for a long while, and he'll move freely in-between them to do an unblockable low sweeping charge attack through them to attack you with little warning.
    15. Just remember the positions of the flames and don't be afraid to keep away from engaging him until you see them go down, and then press the attack. Luckily, besides this new fire sweep, he has no other surprises to speak of, so it comes down to a matter of mastering the moves he throws at you and learning which ones to avoid, counter, or deflect.
    16. Don't be afraid to use items like Divine Grass and Rice to get more healing, or the buffs from Ako or Ungo's Sugar - or even the Ceremonial Tanto to regain Spirit Emblems if you've been shielding a lot. Remember this fight is about getting hits in where you can - but not being afraid to back off and wait to find a way around his big attacks.
    17. When at last you get rid of his last health bar, the man that has become the demon will beg you to put an end and finish him, and you'll get the Memory: Hatred Demon, as well as two Lapis Lazuli!
    18. With the last optional boss defeated, you can also inspect the stairs leading to the nearby watchtower to find a Lump of Grave Wax, and a Dragon's Blood Droplet inside by the Old Woman you can talk to one last time.

    Mini-Boss - Seven Ashina Spears - Shume Masaji Oniwa

    1. To reach the final boss location - you must travel down the main stairway at Ashina Castle and take a left at the Sculptor's Idol to the building where a Samurai General is fighting an Interior Ministry Guard (and you can score Gokan's Sugar nearby). Drop down into the lower level to come out where the Ashina Reservoir Sculptor's Idol is.
    2. The last fight you'll encounter on the way to the final boss is excruciatingly annoying and painful. So much so that if you want to simply bypass it, you can - but you won't be able to fully fill your health bar.
    3. The fight consists of a Seven Ashina Spears miniboss with a companion - a Samurai General who may only have one health bar himself, but can still dish out and take a lot of damage. This fight plays out against the backdrop of several burning areas that can also kill you quickly if you get spaced in the wrong area.
    4. There is no simple strategy to this fight. The Seven Ashina Spears - Shume Masaji Oniwa has a huge reach with his weapon - which means trying to eliminate the Samurai General first is game of cat and mouse as you constantly try and outpace the bigger opponent and hope for a lucky hit on the general before running away. Even if you lock one of them into a Mikiri Counter - the other may just smack you the moment you're done.
    5. There are a few methods you can try to even the odds: There's a wounded Ashina Rifleman sitting by the stairs down - which means you can quickly run over and backstab him and use Pupeteer Ninjutsu to turn him to your side. Why is this useful? Even if the Rifleman does zero damage, he's a great distraction for the mini-boss while you tag the general and lead him away to try and quickly kill him before the spear boss realized what's going on - after all, the puppet can't be killed that quickly.
    6. Another more sneaky method involves bypassing the fight by running out onto the branch and then grappling up to the higher level - this should prompt the pair to run up to the higher platform, but they can't get past the burning wreckage. If you run far enough away, they'll eventually start walking back to their starting position, and you may have enough time to run up behind them and stab the general in the back.
    7. The tree branch itself can be a bit glitchy and may work on your favor. It extends from the platform the fight takes place on, but neither enemy really wants to climb onto it - and usually their pathing won't allow it. However, you might get lucky and have the Samurai General step onto the branch, at which point it becomes very easy for him to fall off - leaving only the Mini- boss left. However, if the Seven Ashina Spears falls off, it won't count as a true win: You won't get a reward, and he'll respawn when you rest.
    8. However you decide to tackle this mission is up to you - but going head to head is certainly the hardest and near impossible option, so if you do, try to use your Shinobi Tools to their full effect and stun or distract enemies, or use the Mist Raven to warp behind your foe for a free hit.
    9. Defeating the Seven Ashina Spears - Shume Masaji Oniwa will reward you with the final Prayer Bead needed to get a full set of 10 Prayer Necklaces and max out your health, which you'll be needing to make use of soon enough. Be sure to also loot the Ministry Dousing Powder and Fulminated Mercury, and then use the branch to grapple up on the path to the moat.

    Near Secret Passage

    1. Make your way to the moat and travel under the bridge, and look for a grapple point on the side of the bridge to find a Pellet, and then move to where you first had Lord Kuro escape from. Use the Secret Passage Key and then commune with the new Sculptor's Idol.
    2. This is it - the path ahead leads to the final boss, and there's nothing left from here on out. Be sure you've done everything you need to, get all your Gourd Seeds and Prayer Beads, unlock whatever Skills you can and upgrade your Prosthetic Tools as much as possible before heading to the final battle, where an old foe waits for one last rematch.

    Boss Fight - Genichiro - Way of Tomoe

    Video Guide: Final Boss Fight

    1. The beginning of this fight puts you in familiar territory: You, Genichiro, and the silvergrass field. However, this time, Genichiro has embraced the Black Mortal Blade, which means he has a few new surprises for you. Still, the more you remember from your last fight, the quicker this portion of the fight can go.
    2. You'll notice that even though Genichiro uses his fighting style from the second half of the fight at Ashina Castle, he doesn't have the power to summon lightning on command (not the right weather perhaps?) and so you'll have one less thing to worry about.
    3. What you will have to worry about is his Black Mortal Blade - which Genichiro will usually start the battle by charging up. Just like your Mortal Draw skill, he'll charge up an extremely long range slash attack that cannot be blocked and will pierce your deflections. He may even charge up an additional slice after the first one. However, the charge up time leaves him extremely vulnerable to being attacked from behind, as he cannot pivot very well after charging up - and you can deal a ton of damage getting in around him by baiting the attack.
    4. Other than baiting out his own Mortal Draw, he may also try to unleash a quick mortal blade attack at the end of his long combo chain, so be ready to race behind him or just avoid his combo entirely - and focus on deflecting his single or double hits, and watch for his lunging thrust or jumping attack that he follows up with a thrust or sweep to destroy his posture quickly. You're going to need to get this phase down to the point that you shouldn't lose any health or waste any items on Genichiro, because he's just the appetizer. Defeat him, and you'll soon find out why.

    Boss Fight - Sword Saint Isshin

    1.
