In an incredible display, Genichiro will use the blood of the Divine Heir on the Mortal Blade to unleash its power, and Isshin Ashina will step forward - only this appears to be a super-powered version of the old man in his prime. This is when the fight starts to get a lot more intense, as Isshin himself has three health bars to Genichiro's one. If you lose to Isshin, you'll have to re- fight Genichiro - so getting the first fight down is key to the rest of this battle.
    2. Isshin may look old and feeble, but he is by no means a slouch, and will employ many of the Ashina Arts in battle against you. He also has a nasty habit of zooming up to you quickly - especially when trying to regain health, so watch out for his quick strike that can knock you off balance. He always follows this up with several space out strikes of his sword - they can deal a lot of damage, but once you have the timing of his single strikes, you can start to reliably deflect, attack, and deflect to look for openings.
    3. At certain points, Isshin may draw backwards and sheath his blade as air whirls around him, heralding a big Ashina Martial Art - which can deal a great amount of damage even if blocked. If you see him pivot to hold his sheathed blade to your left, as he raises it next to his body, he's preparing for a giant vertical slice attack with a huge wave of force - and will fire off a second time. Luckily, this move has a good window to dodge to the side, and then sprint around to attack from the sides while his second attack fires off.
    4. However, if you see him pivot while hiding his blade behind his body, he's preparing a huge whirlwind attack that's even harder to block due its radius on all sides of him. At this point, it's
    5. best to either jump back out of range when you see it coming, or swap to your Loaded Umbrella and soak in the damage to pop up and strike him back.
    6. Isshin also has his standard Ashina Cross slash, where he'll sheath this blade (without the swirling winds), and unleash a strong piercing two-hit cross slash that you'll have to look for a gleam of his blade to see when the attack is coming. However, since it's hard to deflect (and blocking will still hurt you), you can force a fake out by getting close to him. If you do this, he'll suddenly lunge out with a soft quick uppercut strike that you can block, and then spin into a low sweep attack. Forcing him to do this also means you can force him into taking extra posture damage from a jump kick, so you may want to lean into this strategy.
    7. Another perilous attack can come from a quick thrust attack - which he may either launch into after a few single strikes when his blade angles along his arm, or he may start strafing you - a warning sign he's going to ready a quick lunge.
    8. This may seem crazy, but this phase is another fight you're going to want to study meticulously and learn his moves so that you can counter him quickly - baiting out his perilous attacks to punish, and avoiding his martial arts strikes or using the umbrella to make sure you don't take too much damage. This is because the fight is just getting started - and not just because he has multiple health bars.

    Phase Two

    1. Isshin is getting really pumped up now, and will knock you back after losing one health bar, and pulling up a huge and wicked cross spear out of nowhere to wield alongside his blade, and that's not all. He also a gun. Yeah.
    2. Note: Even more so than Genichiro, knowing what moves your opponent is going for is vital in this fight. If you have problems tracking the movements of his sword, spear, and gun - try moving the fight near the cliffs or by the broken bridge where the tall grass can't conceal his movements, and you'll get a lot less surprises this way.
    3. Spear-wielding Isshin loves to engage with his long range spear - usually with a jumping slash, but may also start running up to you before pulling out his gun for a rapid fire burst you'll need to block all the bullets. Sometimes, he'll try to disorient you with a salvo of shots before raising the spear over behind his head for a thrust that you'll want to be ready to Mikiri Counter. Other times, it may be a sweep, so watch where his hands and spear goes.
    4. He'll also use his sword to do a charging slash attack and use his momentum to run past you only to bring the spear up behind him for a follow-up, which he can either turn into an unblockable thrust or move into a combo of slashes with his spear and sword. As for his jumping slash, try to deflect and counter with your own slice - if he dodges back out of range that's fine, otherwise he'll often try to pull out his gun at close range, and slicing him can stop that.
    5. One of his more annoying moves at the end of his combos, or when you start to attack him back, is when he jumps backwards into a retreating swing - you'll only have a moment to react and not chase after him, but deflect it instead, since you won't be able to reach him.
    6. Don't be scared of his new weapons - the game is still the same. Utilize items like Rice, Persimmons, and any buffs you have left to give yourself the edge, and watch his attacks carefully, and don't be afraid to use the Loaded Umbrella for attacks you aren't sure about - like
    7. his 4-hit combo that moves into a charge up sweeping attack, his quick unsheathing attack into two damaging angled slicing waves, or his one-handed Double Ichimonji. You want to be defensively aggressive - meaning you want to try and deflect whatever you can to try and break his posture - and look for the times he tries to lunge to Mikiri Counter and get in some free hits.

    Phase Three

    1. On his final life bar, the ground will tremble with bolts of lightning - hopefully you know where this is going. He'll know use Genichiro's signature leaping lightning attack, bolstered by the huge range of his weapon. It only comes in at an angled slash, so you won't have to worry about straight horizontal, vertical, or beams.
    2. As scary as a lightning-powered Isshin is, it can also be his downfall. When you sense him getting ready to unleash one of his attacks, stand tall before him. Wait until the apxe of his jump when he is about to slice down at you, and jump up and towards him. You should get hit near the apex of your own jump, and then you can slice back before hitting the ground.
    3. This will not only deal a chunk of damage to Isshin's life, but will also stun him for several moments, which is vital for keeping the pressure on him, busting his posture, and getting him that much closer to death.
    4. If you don't manage to reflect his lightning attack, that's alright - but be wary of him pulling out his gun after landing, so be ready to deflect and then press the attack. Take this fight at your own pace and learn his moves carefully to know when he's open - when you can press the attack, and when it's better to deflect his retreating slices to follow up spear swings after his sword strikes.
    5. When at last you take down all three of his health bars, Isshin will fall to the ground and await his execution. Oblige him, and you'll gain the Memory: Saint Isshin, as well as one of his skills - Dragon Flash.

    Ending

    Video - Immortal Severance Ending

    1. At this point, you can return to Lord Kuro where he has fallen in the field, and depending on your choices and what quests you have undertaken, you may have several choices of what to do:
    2. ▪ Give Divine Dragon's Tears - Commence the Immortal Severance Ending
    3. ▪ Give Dragon's Tears and Everblossom - Commence the Purification Ending
    4. ▪ Give Dragon Tears and Frozen Tears - Commence the Return Ending
    5. ▪ See How to Get All Endings
    6. Sit back and enjoy the conclusion to Sekiro. After the story ends, you'll be prompted if you wish to remain in the world, or dive right into New Game Plus. This is your last chance to end any unfinished business, like topping off your health by tracking down any remaining Prayer Beads or Gourd Seeds, or buying whatever items you didn't get from merchants.
